https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:WikiProject\_Socialism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:WikiProject\_Socialism

Democratic socialism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:WikiProject\_Socialism

represents the modernist development of

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism

and its outspoken support for

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy

. The origins of democratic socialism can be traced back to 19th-century

utopian socialist

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utopian\_socialist

thinkers and the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chartism

movement in

Great Britain

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great\_Britain

, which somewhat differed in their goals but shared a common demand of democratic decision making and

public ownership

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public\_ownership

means of production

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Means\_of\_production

, and viewed these as fundamental characteristics of the society they advocated for.

Democratic socialism was also heavily influenced by the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gradualism#Politics\_and\_society

form of socialism promoted by the British

Fabian Society

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabian\_Society

Eduard Bernstein

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduard\_Bernstein

evolutionary socialism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evolutionary\_socialism

In the 19th century, democratic socialism was repressed by many governments; countries such as

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germany

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italy

banned democratic socialist parties.

With the expansion of

liberal democracy

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal\_democracy

universal suffrage

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal\_suffrage

during the 20th century, democratic socialism became a mainstream movement which expanded across the world. Democratic socialists played a major role in liberal democracy,

often forming governing parties or acting as the main

opposition party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opposition\_party

(one major exception being the

United States

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United\_States

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United\_States

19th century

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=1

Background and origins

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=2

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_economics

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Types\_of\_socialism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common\_ownership

public ownership

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public\_ownership

have existed since

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient\_history

Fenner Brockway

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fenner\_Brockway

identified three early democratic socialist groups during the

English Civil War

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English\_Civil\_War

in his book

Britain's First Socialists

, namely the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levellers

, who were pioneers of political democracy and the sovereignty of the people; the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agitators

, who were the pioneers of

participatory control by the ranks at their workplace

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Workers%27\_control

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diggers

, who were pioneers of communal ownership,

cooperation

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooperation

egalitarianism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egalitarianism

The philosophy and tradition of the Diggers and the Levellers was continued in the period described by

E. P. Thompson

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E.\_P.\_Thompson

The Making of the English Working Class

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacobin\_(politics)

groups like the

London Corresponding Society

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London\_Corresponding\_Society

and by polemicists such as

Thomas Paine

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas\_Paine

Their concern for both

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy

social justice

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social\_justice

marked them out as key precursors of democratic socialism.

The first self-conscious socialist movements developed in the 1820s and 1830s. Western European

social critics

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social\_critic

, including

Robert Owen

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert\_Owen

Charles Fourier

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles\_Fourier

Pierre-Joseph Proudhon

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre-Joseph\_Proudhon

Louis Blanc

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis\_Blanc

Charles Hall

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles\_Hall\_(economist)

Henri de Saint-Simon

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henri\_de\_Saint-Simon

, were the first modern socialists who criticised the excessive poverty and inequality generated by the

Industrial Revolution

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial\_Revolution

. The first advocates of socialism favoured social levelling in order to create a

meritocratic

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meritocratic

technocratic

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technocratic

society based on individual talent as opposed to aristocratic privilege. Saint-Simon is regarded as the first individual to coin the term

Saint-Simon was fascinated by the enormous potential of science and technology and advocated a socialist society that would eliminate the disorderly aspects of

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitalism

and would be based on

equal opportunities

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equal\_opportunities

He advocated the creation of a society in which each person was

ranked according to his or her capacities and rewarded according to his or her work

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/To\_each\_according\_to\_his\_contribution

The key focus of Saint-Simon's socialism was on administrative efficiency and industrialism and a belief that science was the key to the progress of human civilisation.

This was accompanied by a desire to implement a rationally organised economy based on planning and geared towards large-scale scientific progress and material progress, embodying a desire for a more directed or

planned economy

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planned\_economy

The term "socialism" was first used in English in the British

Cooperative Magazine

in 1827 and came to be associated with the followers of Owen such as the

Rochdale Pioneers

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rochdale\_Pioneers

, who founded the

co-operative movement

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Co-operative\_movement

. Owen's followers stressed both

participatory democracy

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Participatory\_democracy

and economic socialisation in the form of consumer

co-operatives

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Co-operative

credit unions

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Credit\_unions

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutual\_aid\_(organization)

societies. In the case of the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Owenites

, they also overlapped with a number of other

working-class

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Working-class

labour movements

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour\_movement

such as the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chartism

in the United Kingdom.

Photograph of the Great Chartist Meeting on

Kennington Common

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kennington\_Common

, London, 1848

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chartism

gathered significant numbers around the

People's Charter of 1838

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s\_Charter\_of\_1838

which demanded the extension of

suffrage to all male adults

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal\_manhood\_suffrage

. Leaders in the movement also called for a more equitable distribution of income and better living conditions for the working classes. The very first

trade unions

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trade\_union

consumers' cooperative

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consumers%27\_cooperative

societies also emerged in the hinterland of the Chartist movement as a way of bolstering the fight for these demands.

The Chartists were part of a Europe-wide wave of agitation for social reform and democratic rule, which peaked in the

Revolutions of 1848

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutions\_of\_1848

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France

, the voice of this movement was

La Montagne

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\_Mountain\_(1849)

, also known as the Democratic Socialists.

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl\_Marx

disliked La Montagne, viewing it as a party dominated by the middle class; he called them

Sozialdemokrat

, the first recorded use of the term

social democracy

Around the same time, the British political philosopher

John Stuart Mill

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John\_Stuart\_Mill

also came to advocate a form of economic socialism within a liberal context known as

liberal socialism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal\_socialism

. In later editions of

Principles of Political Economy

(1848), Mill would argue that "as far as economic theory was concerned, there is nothing in principle in economic theory that precludes an economic order based on socialist policies."

Henry George

, a social reformer whose

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoist

movement greatly influenced the development of democratic socialism

Later, the American social reformer

Henry George

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry\_George

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry\_George

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoist

movement influenced the development of democratic socialism,

especially in relation to British socialism

and Fabianism,

along with Mill and the German

historical school of economics

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical\_school\_of\_economics

1880s-1900s

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=3

By the 1880s, these threads had cohered into a socialist movement, with major parties emerging in countries such as Britain and Germany. In 1889 (the centennial of the French Revolution of 1789), the

Second International

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second\_International

was founded, with 384 delegates from twenty countries representing about 300 labour and socialist organisations.

It was termed the Socialist International and

Friedrich Engels

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friedrich\_Engels

was elected honorary president at the third congress in 1893.

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchists

were ejected and not allowed in mainly due to pressure from

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxists

Anarchist writer

George Woodcock

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George\_Woodcock

has argued that at some point the Second International turned "into a battleground over the issue of

libertarian

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libertarian\_socialism

authoritarian

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarian\_socialism

socialism. Not only did they effectively present themselves as champions of minority rights; they also provoked the German Marxists into demonstrating a dictatorial intolerance which was a factor in preventing the British labour movement from following the Marxist direction indicated by such leaders as

H. M. Hyndman

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry\_Hyndman

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=4

Keir Hardie

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Jameskeirhardie.jpg

, an early democratic socialist who founded the British

Independent Labour Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent\_Labour\_Party

In the United Kingdom, the democratic socialist tradition was represented by

William Morris

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William\_Morris

Socialist League

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_League\_(UK,\_1885)

(founded 1884) and the

Fabian Society

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabian\_Society

(1885) and later the

Independent Labour Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent\_Labour\_Party

Keir Hardie

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keir\_Hardie

in the 1890s, of which writer

George Orwell

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George\_Orwell

would later become a prominent member.

The Fabian Society is a British socialist organisation which was established with the purpose of advancing the principles of socialism via

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gradualism#Politics\_and\_society

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform

The society functions primarily as a

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Think\_tank

and is one of the fifteen

socialist societies

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_society\_(Labour\_Party)

affiliated with the Labour Party. Similar societies exist in Australia (the

Australian Fabian Society

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian\_Fabian\_Society

), in Canada (the

Douglas-Coldwell Foundation

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas-Coldwell\_Foundation

, and the since disbanded

League for Social Reconstruction

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League\_for\_Social\_Reconstruction

) and in New Zealand. The society laid many of the foundations of the

Labour Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour\_Party\_(UK)

and subsequently affected the policies of states emerging from the

decolonisation

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decolonisation

British Empire

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British\_Empire

, most notably India and Singapore. Originally, the Fabian Society was committed to the establishment of a

socialist economy

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_economy

, alongside a commitment to

British imperialism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British\_imperialism

colonialism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonialism

as a progressive and modernising force.

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=5

Eduard Bernstein

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bernstein\_Eduard\_1895.jpg

, a socialist theorist within the

German Social Democratic Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German\_Social\_Democratic\_Party

who proposed that socialism could be achieved by peaceful means through incremental legislative reforms in democratic societies

In Germany, democratic socialism became a prominent movement at the end of the 19th century, when the Eisenach's

Social Democratic Workers' Party of Germany

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social\_Democratic\_Workers%27\_Party\_of\_Germany

merged with Lassalle's

General German Workers' Association

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General\_German\_Workers%27\_Association

in 1875 to form the

Social Democratic Party of Germany

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social\_Democratic\_Party\_of\_Germany

. Reformism arose as an alternative to revolution, with leading social democrat

Eduard Bernstein

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduard\_Bernstein

proposing the concept of evolutionary socialism. Revolutionary socialists, encompassing multiple social and political movements that may define revolution differently from one another, quickly targeted the nascent ideology of reformism and

Rosa Luxemburg

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosa\_Luxemburg

condemned Bernstein's

Evolutionary Socialism

in her 1900 essay titled

Social Reform or Revolution?

The Social Democratic Party of Germany became the largest and most powerful socialist party in Europe despite being an illegal organisation under

Otto von Bismarck

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otto\_von\_Bismarck

Anti-Socialist Laws

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Socialist\_Laws

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Socialist\_Laws

until they were repealed in 1890. In the

1893 German federal election

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1893\_German\_federal\_election

, the party gained about 1,787,000 votes, a quarter of the total votes cast according to Engels. In 1895, the year of his death, Engels highlighted

The Communist Manifesto

s emphasis on winning as a first step the "battle of democracy."

Friedrich Engels

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxist

socialist who attempted to bring closer reformists and revolutionaries

In his introduction to the 1895 edition of Karl Marx's

The Class Struggles in France

, Engels attempted to resolve the division between gradualist

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reformist\_socialism

revolutionary

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutionary\_socialism

socialists in the Marxist movement by declaring that he was in favour of short-term tactics of electoral politics that included gradualist and evolutionary socialist policies while maintaining his belief that

revolutionary

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutionary

seizure of power by the

proletariat

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proletariat

should remain a key goal of the socialist movement. In spite of this attempt by Engels to merge gradualism and revolution, his effort only diluted the distinction of gradualism and revolution and had the effect of strengthening the position of the

revisionists

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revisionism\_(Marxism)

Engels' statements in the French newspaper

in which he argued that "revolution" and the "so-called socialist society" were not fixed concepts, but rather constantly changing social phenomena and said that this made "us [socialists] all evolutionists", increased the public perception that Engels was gravitating towards evolutionary socialism. Engels also wrote that it would be "suicidal" to talk about a revolutionary seizure of power at a time when the historical circumstances favoured a parliamentary road to power which he predicted could happen "as early as 1898."

Engels' stance of openly accepting gradualist, evolutionary and parliamentary tactics while claiming that the historical circumstances did not favour revolution caused confusion among political commentators and the public. Bernstein interpreted this as indicating that Engels was moving towards accepting parliamentary reformist and gradualist stances, but he ignored that Engels' stances were tactical as a response to the particular circumstances at that time and that Engels was still committed to revolutionary socialism. Engels was deeply distressed when he discovered that his introduction to a new edition of

The Class Struggles in France

had been edited by Bernstein and

Karl Kautsky

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl\_Kautsky

in a manner which left the impression that he had become a proponent of a peaceful road to socialism.

On 1 April 1895, four months before his death, Engels responded to Kautsky:

I was amazed to see today in the

an excerpt from my 'Introduction' that had been printed without my knowledge and tricked out in such a way as to present me as a peace-loving proponent of legality [at all costs]. Which is all the more reason why I should like it to appear in its entirety in the

in order that this disgraceful impression may be erased. I shall leave Liebknecht in no doubt as to what I think about it and the same applies to those who, irrespective of who they may be, gave him this opportunity of perverting my views and, what's more, without so much as a word to me about it.

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=6

In Australia, the labourist and socialist movements were gaining traction and the

Australian Labor Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian\_Labor\_Party

(ALP) was formed in

Barcaldine, Queensland

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barcaldine,\_Queensland

in 1891 by striking pastoral workers. In 1889, a minority government led by the party was formed in

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queensland

Anderson Dawson

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anderson\_Dawson

Premier of Queensland

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premier\_of\_Queensland

, where it was founded and was in power for one week, becoming the world's first government led by democratic socialists. The ALP has been the main driving force for workers' rights and the welfare state in Australia, backed by Australian trade unions, in particular the

Australian Workers' Union

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian\_Workers%27\_Union

Brazil’s First Socialist Congress occurred in

Rio de Janeiro

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio\_de\_Janeiro

in 1902. Later that year, in

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o\_Paulo

, another Socialist Congress took place and the

Workers' Socialist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Workers%27\_Socialist\_Party\_(Brazil)&action=edit&redlink=1

(Partido Operário Socialista) was founded, considered to be the first socialist party in Brazil. In 1895, the

Socialist Workers Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Socialist\_Workers\_Party\_(Brazil)&action=edit&redlink=1

(Partido Socialista Operário) was founded. That same year, Silvério Fontes, considered the first Brazilian Marxist, launched the Socialist Center of

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santos,\_S%C3%A3o\_Paulo

, which soon published the socialist magazine

A Questão Social

The Social Question)

and newspaper

O Socialista

The Socialist

Early 20th century

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=7

Early democratic success and development

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=8

In Argentina, the

Socialist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Party\_(Argentina)

was established in the 1890s, being led by

Juan B. Justo

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan\_B.\_Justo

Nicolás Repetto

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s\_Repetto

, among others, becoming the first

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass\_party

in the country and in Latin America. The party affiliated itself with the

Second International

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second\_International

Between 1924 and 1940, it was one of the many socialist party members of the

Labour and Socialist International

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour\_and\_Socialist\_International

(LSI), the forerunner of the present-day

Socialist International

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_International

In 1904, Australians elected

Chris Watson

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris\_Watson

as the first

Prime Minister

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime\_Minister\_of\_Australia

Australian Labor Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian\_Labor\_Party

, becoming the first democratic socialist elected into office. The British

Labour Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour\_Party\_(UK)

first won seats in the

House of Commons

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House\_of\_Commons\_of\_the\_United\_Kingdom

French Socialist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French\_Socialist\_Party\_(1902)

(PSF) was founded in 1902.

The PSF came from the merger of the

possibilist

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Possibilism\_(French\_Socialist)

Federation of the Socialist Workers of France

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Possibilism\_(French\_Socialist)

Jean Allemane

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean\_Allemane

Revolutionary Socialist Workers' Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutionary\_Socialist\_Party\_(France)

(POSR) and some independent socialist politicians like

Jean Jaurès

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean\_Jaur%C3%A8s

, who went on to become the party leader.

Socialist Party of France

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Party\_of\_France\_(1902)

Jules Guesde

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jules\_Guesde

, the PSF supported the principle of the alliance with the non-socialist left in the

Bloc des gauches

. Under pressure from the Second International, the two parties merged into the

French Section of the Workers' International

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French\_Section\_of\_the\_Workers%27\_International

Other socialist parties from around the world who were beginning to gain importance in their national politics in the early 20th century included the

Italian Socialist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian\_Socialist\_Party

Spanish Socialist Workers' Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish\_Socialist\_Workers%27\_Party

Swedish Social Democratic Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish\_Social\_Democratic\_Party

Russian Social Democratic Labour Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian\_Social\_Democratic\_Labour\_Party

Socialist Party of America

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Party\_of\_America

and the Chilean

Socialist Workers' Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Workers%27\_Party\_(Chile)

Eugene V. Debs

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Workers%27\_Party\_(Chile)

, leader and presidential candidate in the early 20th century for the

Socialist Party of America

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Party\_of\_America

The socialist

industrial unionism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial\_unionism

Daniel De Leon

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel\_De\_Leon

in the United States represented another strain of early democratic socialism in this period. It favoured a form of government based on industrial unions, but it also sought to establish a socialist government after winning at the ballot box.

Democratic socialism continued to flourish in the

Socialist Party of America

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Party\_of\_America

, especially under the leadership of

Norman Thomas

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman\_Thomas

The Socialist Party of America was formed in 1901 after a merger between the three-year-old

Social Democratic Party of America

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social\_Democratic\_Party\_of\_America

and disaffected elements of the

Socialist Labor Party of America

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Labor\_Party\_of\_America

which had split from the main organisation in 1899. The Socialist Party of America was also known at various times in its long history as the Socialist Party of the United States (as early as the 1910s) and Socialist Party USA (as early as 1935, most common in the 1960s), but the official party name remained Socialist Party of America.

Eugene V. Debs

twice won over 900,000 votes in the

1912 presidential elections

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1912\_United\_States\_presidential\_election

and increased his portion of the popular vote to over 1,000,000 in the

1920 presidential election

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1920\_United\_States\_presidential\_election

despite being imprisoned for alleged sedition. The Socialist Party of America also elected two

Representatives

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United\_States\_House\_of\_Representatives

Victor L. Berger

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor\_L.\_Berger

Meyer London

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meyer\_London

), dozens of state legislators, more than hundred mayors and countless minor officials.

Furthermore, the city of

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee

has been led by a series of democratic socialist mayors in the early 20th century, namely

Frank Zeidler

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank\_Zeidler

Emil Seidel

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emil\_Seidel

Daniel Hoan

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel\_Hoan

Russian Revolution and aftermath

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=9

Alexander Kerensky

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Karenskiy\_AF\_1917.jpg

, a moderate democratic socialist who led the

Russian Provisional Government

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian\_Provisional\_Government

In February 1917, revolution broke out in Russia in which workers, soldiers and peasants established

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet\_(council)

, the monarchy was forced into exile fell and a

provisional government

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian\_Provisional\_Government

was formed until the election of a

constituent assembly

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constituent\_assembly

Alexander Kerensky

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander\_Kerensky

, a Russian lawyer and revolutionary, became a key political figure in the

Russian Revolution

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian\_Revolution

of 1917. After the

February Revolution

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February\_Revolution

, Kerensky joined the newly formed

Russian Provisional Government

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian\_Provisional\_Government

Minister of Justice

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice\_ministry

Minister of War

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister\_of\_War

and after July as the government's

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List\_of\_heads\_of\_government\_of\_Russia#Provisional\_Government/Russian\_Republic\_(1917)

Minister-Chairman

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List\_of\_heads\_of\_government\_of\_Russia#Provisional\_Government/Russian\_Republic\_(1917)

. A leader of the moderate socialist

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trudoviks

faction of the

Socialist Revolutionary Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Revolutionary\_Party

known as the Labour Group, Kerensky was also the vice-chairman of the powerful

Petrograd Soviet

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrograd\_Soviet

. After failing to sign a peace treaty with the German Empire to exit from World War I which led to massive popular unrest against the government cabinet, Kerensky's government was overthrown on 7 November by the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolshevik

Vladimir Lenin

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir\_Lenin

October Revolution

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October\_Revolution

. Soon after the October Revolution, the

Russian Constituent Assembly

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian\_Constituent\_Assembly

elected Socialist-Revolutionary leader

Victor Chernov

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor\_Chernov

President of a Russian Republic

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List\_of\_heads\_of\_state\_of\_Russia

, but it rejected the Bolshevik proposal that endorsed the Soviet decrees on land, peace and workers' control and acknowledged the power of the Soviets of Workers', Soldiers' and Peasants' Deputies.

As a result of the

1917 Russian Constituent Assembly election

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1917\_Russian\_Constituent\_Assembly\_election

which saw a landslide victory for the Socialist-Revolutionaries, the Bolsheviks declared on the next day that the assembly was elected based on outdated party lists which did not reflect the Socialist Revolutionary Party split into Left and Right Socialist-Revolutionary factions. The

Left Socialist-Revolutionaries

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left\_Socialist-Revolutionaries

were allied with the Bolsheviks.

All-Russian Central Executive Committee

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-Russian\_Central\_Executive\_Committee

of the Soviets promptly dissolved the Russian Constituent Assembly.

International Socialist Commission

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International\_Socialist\_Commission

(ISC) was formed in February 1919 at a meeting in Bern, Switzerland by parties that wanted to resurrect the Second International.

At a conference held on 27 February 1921 in Vienna, parties which did not want to be a part of the

Communist International

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist\_International

or the resurrected Second International formed the

International Working Union of Socialist Parties

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International\_Working\_Union\_of\_Socialist\_Parties

The ISC and the IWUSP eventually joined to form the LSI in May 1923 at a meeting held Hamburg.

Kronstadt rebellion

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kronstadt\_rebellion

represented the highest point of

left-wing uprisings

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left-wing\_uprisings\_against\_the\_Bolsheviks

against the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolsheviks

Left-wing groups which did not agree to the centralisation and abandonment of the soviets by the

Bolshevik Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolshevik\_Party

left-wing uprisings against the Bolsheviks

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left-wing\_uprisings\_against\_the\_Bolsheviks

. Such groups included

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism\_in\_Russia

, Left Socialist-Revolutionaries,

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mensheviks

and Socialist-Revolutionaries.

Amidst this left-wing discontent, the most large-scale events were the workers'

Kronstadt rebellion

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kronstadt\_rebellion

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kronstadt\_rebellion

and the anarchist-led

Makhnovshchina

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makhnovshchina

in Ukraine.

In 1922, the

4th World Congress of the Communist International

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4th\_World\_Congress\_of\_the\_Communist\_International

took up the policy of the

united front

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United\_front

, urging Communists to work with rank and file social democrats while remaining critical of their party leaders, whom they criticised for betraying the working class by supporting the war efforts of their respective capitalist classes. For their part, the social democrats pointed to the dislocation and chaos caused by revolution and later the growing authoritarianism of the

Communist parties

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist\_parties

after they achieved power. When the

Communist Party of Great Britain

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist\_Party\_of\_Great\_Britain

applied to affiliate with the

Labour Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour\_Party\_(UK)

in 1920, it was turned down. On seeing the Soviet Union's growing coercive power in 1923, a dying Lenin stated that Russia had reverted to a "

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bourgeois

tsarist machine

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bourgeois

... barely varnished with socialism."

After Lenin's death in January 1924, the

Communist Party of the Soviet Union

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist\_Party\_of\_the\_Soviet\_Union

, increasingly falling under the control of

Joseph Stalin

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph\_Stalin

, rejected the theory that

socialism could not be built solely in the Soviet Union

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World\_revolution

in favour of the concept of

socialism in one country

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism\_in\_one\_country

In other parts of Europe, many democratic socialist parties were united in the IWUSP in the early 1920s and in the

London Bureau

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London\_Bureau

in the 1930s, along with many other socialists of different tendencies and ideologies. These socialist internationals sought to steer a centrist course between the revolutionaries and the social democrats of the Second International and the perceived anti-democratic Communist International. In contrast, the social democrats of the Second International were seen as insufficiently socialist and had been compromised by their support for World War I. The key movements within the IWUSP were the

Austromarxists

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austromarxists

and the British

Independent Labour Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent\_Labour\_Party

In the early 1920s, the

guild socialism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guild\_socialism

G. D. H. Cole

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G.\_D.\_H.\_Cole

attempted to envision a socialist alternative to Soviet-style

authoritarianism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism

council communism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council\_communism

articulated democratic socialist positions in several respects, notably through renouncing the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanguard\_party

role of the revolutionary party and holding that the system of the Soviet Union was not authentically socialist.

Congress Socialist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress\_Socialist\_Party

(CSP) was a socialist caucus within the

Indian National Congress

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian\_National\_Congress

. It was founded in 1934 by Congress members who rejected what they saw as the anti-rational mysticism of

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gandhi

as well as the sectarian attitude of the

Communist Party of India

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist\_Party\_of\_India

towards the Congress. Influenced by

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabianism

Marxism-Leninism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism-Leninism

, the CSP included advocates of armed struggle or sabotage (such as

Yusuf Meherally

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yusuf\_Meherally

Jayaprakash Narayan

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jayaprakash\_Narayan

Basawon Singh (Sinha)

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basawon\_Singh\_(Sinha)

as well as those who insisted upon

Nonviolent resistance

(such as Acharya

Narendra Deva

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narendra\_Deva

). The CSP advocated decentralized socialism in which co-operatives, trade unions, independent farmers, and local authorities would hold a substantial share of the economic power.

1930s and WWII

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=10

Democratic socialism advanced in many industrial democracies in the 1930s, appealing to workers suffering as a result of the

Great Depression

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great\_Depression

. In Britain's general election of 1929 the Labour Party won 288 seats out of 615 and formed a

minority government

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minority\_government

Great Depression

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great\_Depression\_in\_the\_United\_Kingdom

of that period brought high unemployment and Labour Prime Minister

Ramsay MacDonald

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramsay\_MacDonald

sought to make cuts in order to balance the budget. The trade unions opposed MacDonald's proposed cuts and he split the Labour government to form the

National Government

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK\_National\_Government

of 1931. This experience moved the Labour Party leftward. The

Independent Labour Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent\_Labour\_Party

(ILP) remained outside the Labour Party, although remained affiliated to it. It promoted a "Socialism in our time" platform centred around a

living wage

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Living\_wage

nationalisation

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalisation

. As the Depression deepened, it became increasingly dissatisfied with Labour's gradualism, and disaffiliated in 1932, after which its membership declined and it suffered from a series of splinters.

Also in 1932, the ILP co-founded the London Bureau of left-socialist parties, later called the

International Revolutionary Marxist Centre

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International\_Revolutionary\_Marxist\_Centre

or "Three-and-a-Half International", administered by the ILP and chaired by its leader,

Fenner Brockway

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fenner\_Brockway

, for most of its existence; the main forces in the London Bureau were the ILP and Spain's

Workers' Party of Marxist Unification

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Workers%27\_Party\_of\_Marxist\_Unification

In France, the

French Section of the Workers' International

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French\_Section\_of\_the\_Workers%27\_International

(SFIO) was pulled between right-wing factions promoting a more top-down planned economy (

Neosocialism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neosocialism

Nonconformism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-conformists\_of\_the\_1930s

and left-wing factions urging more revolutionary solutions (

Bataille socialiste

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Bataille\_socialiste&action=edit&redlink=1

Marceau Pivert

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marceau\_Pivert

Gauche révolutionnaire

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Workers\_and\_Peasants\_Socialist\_Party

). The party entered into [[Cartel des Gauches|alliance]] with groups to its right, who formed a coalition government in 1932. In

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canada

Co-operative Commonwealth Federation

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Co-operative\_Commonwealth\_Federation

(CCF) was founded in 1932 as an agrarian socialist party. Its first platform was the

Regina Manifesto

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regina\_Manifesto

, adopted in 1930. The CCF gained popularity among industrial workers throughout the 1930s. In 1944, the

Saskatchewan wing of the party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saskatchewan\_CCF

formed the first Socialist government in a Canadian province and stayed in power until

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964\_Saskatchewan\_general\_election

Norman Thomas

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964\_Saskatchewan\_general\_election

, six-time presidential candidate for the

Socialist Party of America

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Party\_of\_America

In the United States, the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New\_Deal

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New\_Deal

of President

Franklin D. Roosevelt

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin\_D.\_Roosevelt

won mass support leaving socialists unable to gain significant ground.

Norman Thomas

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman\_Thomas

Socialist Party of America

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Party\_of\_America

(SPA) attracted nearly 188,000 votes in his

1936 run for president

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S.\_presidential\_election,\_1936

, but performed poorly in historic strongholds of the party and the Socialist Party of America's membership had begun to decline.

Meanwhile, the Trotskyist movement, followed a policy known as the

French Turn

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French\_Turn

, a strategy of

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entryism

in democratic socialist parties such as the SPA, SFIO and ILP, pulling these to the left in some places.

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascism

was also advancing. In Germany, it was the fascists of

Adolf Hitler

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolf\_Hitler

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi\_Party

who successfully exploited the Depression to win power, in January 1933. Hitler's regime swiftly destroyed both the

German Communist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist\_Party\_of\_Germany

and the Social Democratic Party, the worst blow the world socialist movement had ever suffered. This forced Stalin to reassess his strategy, and after its

7th World Congress

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7th\_World\_Congress\_of\_the\_Comintern

in 1935 the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist\_International

began urging a

popular front

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular\_front

against fascism. The socialist parties were at first suspicious, given the bitter hostility of the 1920s, but eventually effective Popular Fronts were formed in both France and Spain. In Italy, the

Italian Socialist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian\_Socialist\_Party

(PSI) established an alliance with the Communists and it gradually cooperated with conservative reformists, revolutionaries, and syndicalists.

In France, the

Popular Front

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular\_Front\_(France)

, headed by the SFIO's

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%A9on\_Blum

3 May 1936 election

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1936\_French\_legislative\_election

, leading to a government composed of Radical and Socialist ministers. On 8 June 1936, the

Matignon Accords

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matignon\_Agreements\_(1936)

granted the 40 hours workweek to the workers, as well as right of

collective bargaining

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective\_bargaining

strike action

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strike\_action

, and dismantled all laws preventing organization of trade-unions.

Elsewhere, democratic socialists remained hostile to Stalinism and rejected the popular front strategy. For example, the

Communist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CPUSA

Earl Russell Browder

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earl\_Russell\_Browder

offered to be Thomas'

running mate

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Running\_mate

on a joint Socialist Party–Communist Party ticket, but Thomas rejected this overture.

After the election of a Popular Front government in Spain in 1936 a fascist military revolt led to the

Spanish Civil War

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish\_Civil\_War

. Democratic socialists from around the world fought on the side of the Republican government, for instance in the

ILP Contingent

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ILP\_Contingent

International Brigades

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International\_Brigades

George Orwell

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George\_Orwell

The crisis in Spain also brought down Blum's Popular Front government in France and ultimately the Popular Fronts were not able to prevent the spread of fascism or the aggressive plans of the fascist powers.

Mid-20th century

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=11

Post-war governments

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=12

Clement Attlee

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Clement\_Attlee.png

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour\_Party\_(UK)

Prime Minister of the United Kingdom

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour\_Party\_(UK)

After World War II, democratic socialist, labourist and social-democratic governments introduced

social reforms

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social\_reform

wealth redistribution

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wealth\_redistribution

welfare state

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare\_state

social programmes

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare\_state

progressive taxation

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive\_taxation

. Those parties dominated post-war politics in the Nordic countries and countries such as Belgium, Czechoslovakia, France, Italy and the United Kingdom. At one point, France claimed the world's most state-controlled capitalist country, starting a period of unprecedented economic growth known as the

Trente Glorieuses

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trente\_Glorieuses

, part of the

post-war economic boom

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-war\_economic\_boom

set in motion by the

Keynesian consensus

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keynesian\_consensus

. The public utilities and industries nationalised by the French government included

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air\_France

Bank of France

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank\_of\_France

Charbonnages de France

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charbonnages\_de\_France

Électricité de France

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89lectricit%C3%A9\_de\_France

Gaz de France

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaz\_de\_France

Régie Nationale des Usines Renault

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C3%A9gie\_Nationale\_des\_Usines\_Renault

Outside Europe, the

Japan Socialist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan\_Socialist\_Party

was briefly in power in 1947.

In 1945, the British

Labour Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour\_Party\_(UK)

Clement Attlee

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clement\_Attlee

was elected to office based on a radical, democratic socialist programme. The Labour government nationalised major public utilities and industries such as mining, gas, coal, electricity,

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British\_Rail

, iron, steel and the

Bank of England

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank\_of\_England

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BP

was nationalised in 1951.

Anthony Crosland

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony\_Crosland

stated that at least 25% of British industry was nationalised and that public employees, including those in nationalised industries, constituted a similar proportion of the country's total workforce.

The 1964–1970 and 1974–1979 Labour governments strengthened the policy of nationalisation.

These Labour governments renationalised steel (

British Steel

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British\_Steel\_(1967-1999)

) in 1967 after the

Conservatives

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative\_Party\_(UK)

had privatised it and nationalised car production (

British Leyland

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British\_Leyland

The 1945–1951 Labour government also established

National Health Service

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National\_Health\_Service

which provided taxpayer-funded health care to Every British citizen, free at the point of use.

High-quality housing for the working class was provided in

council housing

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council\_housing

estates and university education became available to every citizen via a school grant system.

The 1945–1951 Labour government has been described as being transformative democratic socialist.

Einar Gerhardsen

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour\_Party\_(Norway)

Prime Minister of Norway

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour\_Party\_(Norway)

During most of the post-war era, democratic socialist, labourist and social-democratic parties dominated the political scene and laid the ground to

universalistic welfare states

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare\_state#Forms

in the Nordic countries.

For much of the mid- and late 20th century, Sweden was governed by the

Swedish Social Democratic Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish\_Social\_Democratic\_Party

largely in cooperation with

trade unions

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish\_Trade\_Union\_Confederation

and industry.

Tage Erlander

was the leader of the Social Democratic Party and led the government from 1946 until 1969, an uninterrupted tenure of twenty-three years, one of the longest in any democracy. From 1945 until 1962, the

Norwegian Labour Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian\_Labour\_Party

held an absolute majority in the parliament led by

Einar Gerhardsen

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Einar\_Gerhardsen

, who served Prime Minister for seventeen years. The Danish

Social Democrats

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social\_Democrats\_(Denmark)

governed Denmark for most of the 20th century and since the 1920s and through the 1940s and the 1970s a large majority of Prime Ministers were members of the Social Democrats, the largest and most popular political party in Denmark.

Social Democratic

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish\_Social\_Democratic\_Party

Prime Minister of Sweden

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish\_Social\_Democratic\_Party

This particular adaptation of the

mixed economy

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed\_economy

, better known as the

Nordic model

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic\_model

, is characterised by more generous

welfare states

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare\_state

(relative to other

developed countries

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developed\_countries

) which are aimed specifically at enhancing individual autonomy, ensuring the universal provision of basic human rights and stabilising the economy. It is distinguished from other welfare states with similar goals by its emphasis on maximising labour force participation, promoting gender equality, egalitarian and extensive benefit levels, large magnitude of redistribution and expansionary fiscal policy.

In the 1950s,

popular socialism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular\_socialism

emerged as a vital current of the left in

Nordic countries

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic\_countries

could be characterised as a democratic socialism in the same vein as it placed itself between

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism

social democracy

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social\_democracy

In the 1960s, Gerhardsen established a planning agency and tried to establish a planned economy.

Prominent Swedish Prime Minister

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olof\_Palme

identified himself as a democratic socialist.

Rehn–Meidner model

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rehn%E2%80%93Meidner\_model

was adopted by the Swedish Social Democratic Party in the late 1940s. This economic model allowed capitalists who owned very productive and efficient firms to retain excess profits at the expense of the firm's workers, exacerbating income inequality and causing workers in these firms to agitate for a better share of the profits in the 1970s. Women working in the state sector also began to assert pressure for better and equal wages.

In 1976, economist

Rudolf Meidner

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudolf\_Meidner

established a study committee that came up with a proposal called the Meidner Plan which entailed the transferring of the excess profits into investment funds controlled by the workers in said efficient firms, with the goal that firms would create further employment and pay workers higher wages in return rather than unduly increasing the wealth of company owners and managers.

Capitalists immediately denounced the proposal as socialism and launched an unprecedented opposition and smear campaign against it, threatening to terminate the class compromise established in the 1938

Saltsjöbaden Agreement

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saltsj%C3%B6baden\_Agreement

Anti-colonialism and revolutions

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=13

Hungarian Revolution of 1956

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian\_Revolution\_of\_1956

represented a breaking point in the wider socialist movement

Hungarian Revolution of 1956

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian\_Revolution\_of\_1956

was a spontaneous nationwide

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolt

by democratic socialists against the

Marxist–Leninist

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxist%E2%80%93Leninist

government of the

People's Republic of Hungary

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s\_Republic\_of\_Hungary

and its policies of repression, lasting from 23 October until 10 November 1956.

Soviet leader

Nikita Khrushchev

denunciation of the excesses of Stalin's regime

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/On\_the\_Cult\_of\_Personality\_and\_Its\_Consequences

20th Congress of the Communist Party of the Soviet Union

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20th\_Congress\_of\_the\_Communist\_Party\_of\_the\_Soviet\_Union

that same year

as well as the revolt in Hungary produced ideological fractures and disagreements within the democratic communist and socialist parties of Western Europe. A split ensued within the

Italian Communist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian\_Communist\_Party

(PCI), with most ordinary members and the PCI leadership, including

Giorgio Napolitano

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgio\_Napolitano

Palmiro Togliatti

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmiro\_Togliatti

, regarding the Hungarian insurgents as counter-revolutionaries as reported in

, the official PCI newspaper.

Giuseppe Di Vittorio

, General Secretary of the

Italian General Confederation of Labour

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian\_General\_Confederation\_of\_Labour

, repudiated the leadership position, as did the prominent party members

Loris Fortuna

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loris\_Fortuna

Antonio Giolitti

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio\_Giolitti

and many other influential communist intellectuals who later were expelled or left the party.

Pietro Nenni

, the national secretary of the

Italian Socialist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian\_Socialist\_Party

, a close ally of the PCI, opposed the Soviet intervention as well.

Napolitano, elected in 2006 as

President of the Italian Republic

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President\_of\_the\_Italian\_Republic

, wrote in his 2005 political autobiography that he regretted his justification of Soviet action in Hungary and that at the time he believed in party unity and the international leadership of Soviet communism.

Jawaharlal Nehru

, a prominent

Third World socialist

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third\_World\_socialist

Prime Minister of India

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime\_Minister\_of\_India

Indian National Congress

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian\_National\_Congress

Communist Party of Great Britain

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist\_Party\_of\_Great\_Britain

, dissent that began with the repudiation of Stalin by

John Saville

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John\_Saville

E. P. Thompson

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E.\_P.\_Thompson

, influential historians and members of the

Communist Party Historians Group

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist\_Party\_Historians\_Group

, culminated in a loss of thousands of party members as events unfolded in Hungary.

Peter Fryer

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter\_Fryer

, correspondent for the party newspaper

The Daily Worker

, reported on the violent suppression of the uprising, but his dispatches were heavily censored. Fryer resigned from the paper upon his return and was later expelled from the party.

In France, moderates such as historian

Emmanuel Le Roy Ladurie

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmanuel\_Le\_Roy\_Ladurie

resigned, questioning the policy of supporting Soviet actions by the

French Communist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French\_Communist\_Party

. The French anarchist philosopher and writer

Albert Camus

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert\_Camus

open letter

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open\_letter

The Blood of the Hungarians

, criticising the West's lack of action.

Jean-Paul Sartre

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Paul\_Sartre

, still a determined party member, criticised the Soviets.

Jayaprakash Narayan

, an anti-totalitarian socialist and democratic socialist influence, among members of the

Congress Socialist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress\_Socialist\_Party

In the post-war years, socialism became increasingly influential throughout the so-called

Third World

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third\_World

decolonisation

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decolonisation

. During India's

freedom movement

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian\_independence\_movement

and fight for independence, many figures in the leftist faction of the

Indian National Congress

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian\_National\_Congress

organised themselves as the

Congress Socialist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress\_Socialist\_Party

. Their politics and those of the early and intermediate periods of

Jayaprakash Narayan

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jayaprakash\_Narayan

's career combined a commitment to the socialist transformation of society with a principled opposition to the one-party authoritarianism they perceived in the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stalinist

In Africa, many independence movements (e.g. in

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senegal

, under the leadership of

Léopold Sédar Senghor

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%A9opold\_S%C3%A9dar\_Senghor

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghana

under the leadership of

Kwame Nkrumah

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kwame\_Nkrumah

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinea

Ahmed Sékou Touré

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed\_S%C3%A9kou\_Tour%C3%A9

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanzania

, under the leadership of

Julius Nyerere

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius\_Nyerere

, who developed the concept of

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ujamaa

, co-operative economoics) were heavily influenced by democratic socialism, although often drifting away from democracy after taking power.

Embracing a new ideology called

Third World socialism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third\_World\_socialism

, countries in Africa, Asia and Latin America often nationalised industries held by foreign owners. In addition, the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New\_Left

, a movement composed of activists, educators, agitators and others who sought to implement a broad range of social reforms on issues such as gay rights, abortion, gender roles and drugs,

in contrast to earlier leftist or Marxist movements that had taken a more

vanguardist

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanguardist

approach to social justice and focused mostly on

labour unionisation

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour\_unionisation

and issues related to

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social\_class

, became prominent in the 1960s and 1970s.

The New Left rejected involvement with the

labour movement

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour\_movement

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism

's historical theory of

class struggle

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Class\_struggle

The 1960s and the New Left

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=14

Martin Luther King Jr.

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Martin\_Luther\_King,\_Jr..jpg

, US civil rights leader who stated his support for democratic socialism

In the United States, the New Left was associated with the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-war\_movement

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hippie

movements as well as the

black liberation

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black\_Power

movements such as the

Black Panther Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black\_Panther\_Party

While initially formed in opposition to the so-called

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old\_Left

Democratic Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic\_Party\_(United\_States)

, groups composing the New Left gradually became central players in the Democratic coalition, culminating in the nomination of the outspoken

anti-Vietnam War

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opposition\_to\_United\_States\_involvement\_in\_the\_Vietnam\_War

George McGovern

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opposition\_to\_United\_States\_involvement\_in\_the\_Vietnam\_War

Democratic Party primaries

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972\_Democratic\_Party\_presidential\_primaries

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972\_Democratic\_Party\_presidential\_primaries

1972 United States presidential election

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972\_United\_States\_presidential\_election

US civil rights leader

Martin Luther King Jr.

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin\_Luther\_King\_Jr.

supported the ideals of

democratic socialism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic\_socialism

, although he was reluctant to speak directly of this support due to the

anti-communist

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-communist

sentiment being projected throughout the United States at the time, and the association of socialism with

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism

. King believed that capitalism could not adequately provide the necessities of many American people, particularly the African-American community.

King expressed that "the evils of capitalism are as real as the evils of militarism and evils of racism".

protest wave of 1968

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protest\_wave\_of\_1968

represented a worldwide escalation of social conflicts, predominantly characterised by popular rebellions against military dictatorships, capitalists and bureaucratic elites, who responded with an escalation of

political repression

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political\_repression

authoritarianism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism

. These protests marked a turning point for the

civil rights movement

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil\_rights\_movement

in the United States which produced revolutionary movements like the Black Panther Party. The prominent civil rights leader

Martin Luther King Jr.

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin\_Luther\_King\_Jr.

organised the

Poor People's Campaign

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poor\_People%27s\_Campaign

to address issues of economic and social justice

while personally showing sympathy with democratic socialism.

The classic

Port Huron Statement

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port\_Huron\_Statement

Students for a Democratic Society

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Students\_for\_a\_Democratic\_Society

combined a stringent critique of the Stalinist model with calls for a democratic socialist reconstruction of society.

In reaction to the

Tet Offensive

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tet\_Offensive

, protests also sparked a broad movement in opposition to the

Vietnam War

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam\_War

all over the United States and even into London, Paris, Berlin and Rome. Mass socialist or communist movements grew not only in the United States, but also in most European countries. The most spectacular manifestation of this was the

May 1968 protests in France

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May\_1968\_protests\_in\_France

in which students linked up with strikes of up to ten million workers and the movement seemed capable of overthrowing the government, albeit for only a few days. In many other capitalist countries, struggles against dictatorships, state repression and colonisation were also marked by protests in 1968 such as the beginning of

the Troubles

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\_Troubles

in Northern Ireland, the

Tlatelolco massacre

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tlatelolco\_massacre

in Mexico City and the escalation of guerrilla warfare against the

military dictatorship in Brazil

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military\_dictatorship\_in\_Brazil

Countries governed by Marxist–Leninist parties had protests against bureaucratic and military elites. In Eastern Europe, there were widespread protests that escalated particularly in the

Prague Spring

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prague\_Spring

in Czechoslovakia.

In response, the Soviet Union occupied Czechoslovakia, but the occupation was denounced by the Italian and French communist parties as well as the

Communist Party of Finland

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist\_Party\_of\_Finland

Salvador Allende

President of Chile

and member of the

, whose presidency and life were ended by a

military coup

In Latin America,

liberation theology

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation\_theology

is a socialist tendency within the

Roman Catholic Church

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman\_Catholic\_Church

that emerged in the 1960s.

Salvador Allende

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvador\_Allende

, a physician and candidate for the

Socialist Party of Chile

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Party\_of\_Chile

, became the first democratically elected Marxist

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President\_of\_Chile

presidential elections were held in 1970

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970\_Chilean\_presidential\_election

. However, his government was ousted three years later in a

military coup

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973\_Chilean\_coup\_d%27%C3%A9tat

backed by the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA

and the United States government, instituting the right-wing

dictatorship

Augusto Pinochet

which lasted until the late 1980s.

In addition,

Michael Manley

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael\_Manley

, a self-described democratic socialist, served as the fourth

Prime Minister of Jamaica

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime\_Minister\_of\_Jamaica

from 1972 to 1980 and from 1989 to 1992. According to opinion polls, he remains one of Jamaica's most popular Prime Ministers since independence.

Eurocommunism

became a trend in the 1970s and 1980s in various Western European communist parties

which intended to develop a modernised theory and practice of social transformation that was more relevant for a Western European country and less aligned to the influence or control of the

Communist Party of the Soviet Union

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist\_Party\_of\_the\_Soviet\_Union

Outside of Western Europe, it is sometimes referred to as neocommunism.

Some communist parties with strong popular support, notably the

Italian Communist Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian\_Communist\_Party

Communist Party of Spain

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist\_Party\_of\_Spain

, enthusiastically adopted Eurocommunism and the

Communist Party of Finland

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist\_Party\_of\_Finland

was dominated by Eurocommunists.

In the late 1970s and in the 1980s, the

Socialist International

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_International

had extensive contacts and held discussion with the two powers of the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold\_War

, the United States and the Soviet Union, regarding the relations between the East and West, along with arms control. Since then, the Socialist International has admitted as member parties the Nicaraguan

Sandinista National Liberation Front

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandinista\_National\_Liberation\_Front

and the left-wing

Puerto Rican Independence Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerto\_Rican\_Independence\_Party

as well as former communist parties such as the Italian

Democratic Party of the Left

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic\_Party\_of\_the\_Left

Front for the Liberation of Mozambique

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FRELIMO

. The Socialist International aided social democratic parties in re-establishing themselves after right-wing dictatorships were toppled in

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnation\_Revolution

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish\_transition\_to\_democracy

, respectively in 1974 and 1975. Until its 1976 congress in Geneva, the Socialist International had few members outside Europe and no formal involvement with Latin America.

In the United States, the

Social Democrats, USA

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social\_Democrats,\_USA

, an association of reformist social democrats and democratic socialists, was founded in 1972. The

Socialist Party of America

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Party\_of\_America

had stopped running independent presidential candidates and begun reforming itself towards democratic socialism. Consequently, the party's name was changed because it had confused the public. With the name change in place, the Social Democrats, USA clarified its vision to Americans who confused democratic socialism with

Marxism–Leninism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism%E2%80%93Leninism

, harshly opposed by the organisation.

In 1983, the

Democratic Socialists of America

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic\_Socialists\_of\_America

was founded as a merger of the

Democratic Socialist Organizing Committee

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic\_Socialist\_Organizing\_Committee

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic\_Socialist\_Organizing\_Committee

New American Movement

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New\_American\_Movement

an organization of New Left veterans.

Earlier in 1973,

Michael Harrington

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael\_Harrington

Irving Howe

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irving\_Howe

formed the Democratic Socialist Organizing Committee which articulated a democratic socialist message

while a smaller faction associated with peace activist

David McReynolds

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David\_McReynolds

Socialist Party USA

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Party\_USA

Harrington and the

socialist-feminist

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist-feminist

Barbara Ehrenreich

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbara\_Ehrenreich

were elected as the first co-chairs of the organisation

which does not stand its own candidates in elections and instead "fights for reforms ... that will weaken the power of corporations and increase the power of working people."

In Greece, the Panhellenic Socialist Movement, better known as

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PASOK

, was founded on 3 September 1974 by

Andreas Papandreou

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andreas\_Papandreou

as a democratic socialist,

left-wing nationalist

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left-wing\_nationalism

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venizelist

and social democratic

party following the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metapolitefsi

military dictatorship of 1967–1974

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek\_military\_junta\_of\_1967%E2%80%931974

As a result of the

1981 legislative election

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1981\_Greek\_legislative\_election

, PASOK became Greece's first

centre-left

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre-left\_politics

party to win a majority in the

Hellenic Parliament

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hellenic\_Parliament

and the party would later pass several important economic and social reforms that would reshape Greece in the years ahead until its collapse in the 2010s.

Mikhail Gorbachev

General Secretary of the Communist Party of the Soviet Union

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General\_Secretary\_of\_the\_Communist\_Party\_of\_the\_Soviet\_Union

, who wanted to move the Soviet Union towards social democracy

During the 1980s, Soviet leader

Mikhail Gorbachev

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail\_Gorbachev

intended to move the Soviet Union towards

Nordic-style social democracy

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic\_model

calling it a "socialist beacon for all mankind."

Opposition to neoliberalism and Third Way

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=History\_of\_democratic\_socialism&action=edit&section=17

Michael Foot

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Michael\_Foot\_(1981).jpg

Leader of the Labour Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leader\_of\_the\_Labour\_Party\_(UK)

Many social-democratic parties, particularly after the Cold War, adopted

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoliberal

economic policies,

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austerity

deregulation

economic liberalism

This resulted in the rise of more left-wing and democratic socialist parties that rejected neoliberalism and the Third Way.

In the United Kingdom, prominent democratic socialists within the

Labour Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour\_Party\_(UK)

Michael Foot

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael\_Foot

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony\_Benn

put forward democratic socialism into an actionable manifesto during the 1970s and 1980s, but this was voted overwhelmingly against in the

1983 general election

Margaret Thatcher

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margaret\_Thatcher

and the manifesto was referred to as "

the longest suicide note in history

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\_longest\_suicide\_note\_in\_history

By the 1980s, with the rise of conservative neoliberal politicians such as

Ronald Reagan

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald\_Reagan

in the United States, Margaret Thatcher in Britain,

Brian Mulroney

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian\_Mulroney

in Canada and

Augusto Pinochet

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augusto\_Pinochet

in Chile, the Western

welfare state

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare\_state

was attacked from within, but state support for the corporate sector was maintained.

According to

Kristen Ghodsee

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kristen\_Ghodsee

, the triumphalist attitudes of Western powers at the end of the Cold War and the fixation with linking all leftist and socialist ideals with the excesses of

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stalinism

allowed neoliberalism to fill the void. This undermined democratic institutions and reforms, leaving a trail of economic misery, unemployment, hopelessness and rising economic inequality throughout the former Eastern Bloc and much of the West in the following decades. With democracy weakened and the anti-capitalist left marginalised, the anger and resentment which followed the period of neoliberalism was channeled into extremist nationalist movements in both the former and the latter.

, a leading left-wing

Labour Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour\_Party\_(UK)

As a result of the party's shift,

Labour Party leader

Neil Kinnock

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neil\_Kinnock

made a public attack against the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entryist

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Militant\_(Trotskyist\_group)

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Militant\_(Trotskyist\_group)

at the 1985 Labour Party conference in

Bournemouth

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bournemouth

The Labour Party ruled that Militant was ineligible for affiliation with the Labour Party and the party gradually expelled Militant supporters.

The Kinnock leadership had refused to support the

1984–1985 miner's strike

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK\_miners%27\_strike\_(1984%E2%80%9385)

over pit closures,

a decision that the party's left-wing and the

National Union of Mineworkers

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National\_Union\_of\_Mineworkers\_(Great\_Britain)

blamed for the strike's eventual defeat.

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clause\_IV

proposal included for the first time referring to Labour as

democratic socialist

, but whose critics disputed his socialist credentials

In 1989, the Socialist International adopted a new Declaration of Principles at its 18th congress in Stockholm, Sweden, stating: "Democratic socialism is an international movement for freedom, social justice, and solidarity. Its goal is to achieve a peaceful world where these basic values can be enhanced and where each individual can live a meaningful life with the full development of his or her personality and talents, and with the guarantee of human and civil rights in a democratic framework of society."

Within the Labour Party, the

democratic socialist

label was used historically by those who identified with the tradition represented by the

Independent Labour Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent\_Labour\_Party

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soft\_left

of non-Marxist socialists such as Michael Foot around the

magazine and some of the

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard\_left

Campaign Group

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campaign\_Group

around Tony Benn.

The Campaign Group, along with the

Socialist Society

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Society

Raymond Williams

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raymond\_Williams

and others, formed the

Socialist Movement

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist\_Movement

in 1987 which now produces the magazine

In the late 1990s, the Labour Party under the leadership of

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony\_Blair

enacted policies based on the liberal

market economy

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market\_economy

with the intention of delivering public services via the

private finance initiative

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private\_finance\_initiative

. Influential in these policies was the idea of a

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third\_Way

which called for a re-evaluation and reduction of welfare state policies.

In 1995, the Labour Party re-defined its position on socialism by re-wording

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clause\_IV

Constitution

https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Labour\_Party\_Rule\_Book&action=edit&redlink=1

, effectively removing all references to public, direct worker or municipal ownership of the means of production and now reading: "The Labour Party is a democratic socialist party. It believes that, by the strength of our common endeavour we achieve more than we achieve alone, so as to create, for each of us, the means to realise our true potential, and, for all of us, a community in which power, wealth, and opportunity are in the hands of the many, not the few."

New Labour eventually won the

1997 United Kingdom general election

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997\_United\_Kingdom\_general\_election

in a landslide and Blair described New Labour as a "left of centre party, pursuing economic prosperity and social justice as partners and not as opposites."

It has been argued that the Labour Party under the

Blair ministry

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First\_Blair\_ministry

effectively governed from the

radical centre

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radical\_centrism

, something which Blair had promised to do in the 1997 general election.

https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoliberal

From 2005 until 2007, the country was wracked by thousands of protests from poor working-class communities. One of these gave rise to a mass democratic socialist movement of shack dwellers called

Abahlali baseMjondolo

which continues to work for popular people's planning and against the proliferation of capitalism in land and housing.

United Front
